\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}&\xrightarrow{\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\simeq$}\vskip-2.0pt}}&\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}\\
\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)&\mapsto&\big([x_{0}:z^{c}\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}x_{1}+x_{0}\tilde{P}(y_{0},y_{1},z);z^{a}y_{0}:y_{1}],\frac{1}{z}\big),\text{ with}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\tilde{P}(y_{0},y_{1},z)&=&\lambda_{2}P(y_{0},y_{1},z)+\lambda_{2}Q_{1}(y_{0},y_{1},z)z^{c}+Q_{2}\big(y_{0}z^{a},y_{1},\frac{1}{z}\big).\end{array}

To get a transition function of the form
\nu_{c^{\prime},P^{\prime}}
, we then need
c^{\prime}=c
and
\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}=1
.
∎

From now on we write
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}=\{f\in\mathrm{k}[z]\mid\deg(f)\leq r\}=\mathrm{k}\oplus\mathrm{k}z\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathrm{k}z^{r}
.

Corollary 3.3.7
.

Let
a,b,c\in\mathbb{Z}
, with
a,b\geq 0
and with
c\leq 0
if
b=0
.

(1)

There is a unique isomorphism class of decomposable
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
with numerical invariants
(a,b,c)
, represented by
\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
.

(2)

If
b=0
or
c\leq 1
, every
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
with numerical invariants
(a,b,c)
is decomposable, and thus isomorphic to
\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
.

(3)

If
b\geq 1
and
c\geq 2
, every
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle with numerical invariants
(a,b,c)
is isomorphic to
Z_{a}^{b,c,P}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
, where

\begin{array}[]{c}P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}{y_{0}}^{i}{y_{1}}^{b-i}P_{i}(z)z^{ai+1}\end{array}

and
P_{i}(z)\in\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai}

(
hence
P_{i}=0
if
c<ai+2)
, for
i=0,\ldots,b
.

The isomorphism class of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle is determined by the class of
P
, up to scalar multiplication by an element of
\,\mathrm{k}^{*}
. The
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle is decomposable if and only if
P=0
.

Proof.

Assertion
(1)
has already been proven (Remark
3.3.2
).
If
b=0
, then Proposition
3.3.1
shows that every
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle over
\mathbb{F}_{a}
with numerical invariants
(a,b,c)
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}=\mathcal{F}_{a}^{0,c}
. We can thus assume
b\geq 1
.

Proposition
3.3.1
shows that every
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
with invariants
(a,b,c)
is isomorphic to
Z_{a}^{b,c,P}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
for some
P\in\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1},z,z^{-1}]_{b}.
Lemma
3.3.6
shows that the isomorphism class is inside the set of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles with invariants
(a,b,c)
and corresponds to an equivalence class on
\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1},z,z^{-1}]_{b}
, where
P
and
P^{\prime}
are equivalent if and only if

P^{\prime}=\lambda P+Q_{1}(y_{0},y_{1},z)z^{c}+Q_{2}(y_{0}z^{a},y_{1},z^{-1})
, for
\lambda\in\mathrm{k}^{*}
and
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1},z]_{b}
.
In particular, each equivalence class is given by an element

P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}{y_{0}}^{i}{y_{1}}^{b-i}P_{i}(z)z^{ai+1}
,

where
P_{i}(z)\in\mathrm{k}[z]
is of degree at most
c-ai-2
for
i=0,\ldots,b
and the element
P
is unique up to multiplication by
\lambda\in\mathrm{k}^{*}
. If
c\leq 1
, then
P
is zero, so every
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
with numerical invariants
(a,b,c)
is decomposable. This achieves the proof.
∎

Corollary 3.3.8
.

Let
a,b,c\in\mathbb{Z}
, with
a\geq 0
,
b\geq 1
, and
c\geq 2
. The isomorphism classes of non-decomposable
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
with numerical invariants
(a,b,c)
are parametrised by the projective space

\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{M}_{a}^{b,c}=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigoplusop\displaylimits\limits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}\cdot\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai}\right).\end{array}

Proof.

This follows from Corollary
3.3.7
(3)
.
∎

Remark 3.3.9
.

We have
\mathcal{M}_{a}^{b,c}\simeq\mathbb{P}^{\frac{1}{2}(d+1)(2(c-1)-ad)-1}
, where
d
is the biggest integer such that
d\leq b
and
ad\leq c-2
(and is thus equal to
b
if
ab\leq c-2
). Indeed, the dimension of the vector space
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai}
is equal to
c-1-ai
if
ai\leq c-2
and to
0
if
ai>c-2
. Hence, the dimension of
\bigoplusop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}\cdot\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai}
is equal to
\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{d}(c-1-ai)=\frac{1}{2}(d+1)(2(c-1)-ad)
.

3.4. Action of
\boldsymbol{\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})}
on the moduli spaces
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}_{a}^{b,c}}

If
\pi\colon X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle with numerical invariants
(a,b,c)
, then so is
\varphi\circ\pi\colon X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
for each
\varphi\in\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
. Indeed, the action of
\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
does not change the exact sequence of Definition
1.4.1
. This then gives a natural left-action of
\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
on
\mathcal{M}_{a}^{b,c}
, that we describe in this section; it will be very useful later, for the following reason: a point
[p\colon X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}]\in\mathcal{M}_{a}^{b,c}
is fixed by
g\in\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
if and only if
g
lifts to an automorphism of the variety
X
.

Since we have a group homomorphism
\GL_{2}\to\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
(see
\lx@sectionsign\ref{Subsec:AutFa}
), we get an action of
\GL_{2}
on
\mathcal{M}_{a}^{b,c}=\mathbb{P}\big(\bigoplusop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}\cdot\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai}\big)
(see Corollary
3.3.8
). We will show that this
\GL_{2}
-action coincides with the following one.

Definition 3.4.1
.

Let
r\geq 0
, and let us equip
V=\mathrm{k}^{2}
with the standard left-action of
\GL_{2}
. There is a unique left-action of
\GL_{2}
on
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}
making the following map
\GL_{2}
-equivariant:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}V&\mapsto&\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}\\
(u,v)&\mapsto&\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{r}u^{i}v^{r-i}\cdot z^{i}.\end{array}

Remark 3.4.2
.

Equipped with the
\GL_{2}
-action of Definition
3.4.1
, the vector space
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}
identifies with the
r
-th symmetric power of the standard representation of
\GL_{2}
. In particular,
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}
is an irreducible
\GL_{2}
-representation (as we assumed
\mathrm{k}
to be of characteristic zero).

We first need the following observations on the action of
\GL_{2}
on
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}
of Definition
3.4.1
.

Lemma 3.4.3
.

Let
r\geq 0
, and let
P\in\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}
.

(1)

If
\sigma=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}\in\GL_{2}
, then
\sigma(P)=P(z^{-1})\cdot z^{r}
.

(2)

If
\sigma=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
0&\delta\end{bmatrix}\in\GL_{2}
, then
\hat{P}:=\sigma(P)\in\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}
is the unique polynomial that satisfies

(3.1)

P(z)=\frac{\alpha}{\delta^{r}(\beta z+\alpha)}\cdot\hat{P}\left(\frac{\delta z}{\beta z+\alpha}\right),

where we identify
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}
with
\mathrm{k}[z]/(z^{r+1})
and compute the above equality in this ring.

Proof.

(1)
The action of
\sigma
on
V
being
(u,v)\mapsto(v,u)
, the action on
P\in\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}
sends
\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{r}a_{i}z^{i}
onto
\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{r}a_{r-i}z^{i}
, which is exactly
P\mapsto P(z^{-1})z^{r}
.

(2)

We first observe that Equation (
3.1
) uniquely determines
\hat{P}
in terms of
P
since
\alpha
,
\delta
, and
\beta z+\alpha
are invertible in
\mathrm{k}[z,z^{-1}]/(z^{r+1})
(because
\alpha\delta\neq 0
). We then check that if Equation (
3.1
) is true for
P,Q\in\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}
, then it is true for all
\mu P+\nu Q
with
\mu,\nu\in\mathrm{k}
. We thus only need to check it for
P=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{r}u^{i}v^{r-i}\cdot z^{i}
, where
(u,v)\in\mathrm{k}^{2}
. By the definition of the action, we get
\hat{P}=\sigma(P)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{r}(\alpha u+\beta v)^{i}(\delta v)^{r-i}\cdot z^{i}
, which yields

\begin{array}[]{rcl}(\beta z+\alpha)^{r+1}\cdot P(z)&=&\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{r}u^{i}v^{r-i}z^{i}(\beta z+\alpha)^{r+1},\\[4.30554pt]
\frac{\alpha(\beta z+\alpha)^{r}}{\delta^{r}}\cdot\hat{P}\left(\frac{\delta z}{\beta z+\alpha}\right)&=&\frac{\alpha(\beta z+\alpha)^{r}}{\delta^{r}}\cdot\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{r}(\alpha u+\beta v)^{i}(\delta v)^{r-i}\cdot\left(\frac{\delta z}{\beta z+\alpha}\right)^{i}\\[4.30554pt]
&=&\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{r}\alpha(\alpha u+\beta v)^{i}(\beta z+\alpha)^{r-i}v^{r-i}z^{i}.\end{array}

Comparing the coefficients of
z^{p}
for
p=0,\ldots,r-1
, we need to prove that

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{p}u^{i}v^{r-i}\bigl(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{r+1}{p-i}\bigr)\beta^{p-i}\alpha^{r+1-(p-i)}&=&\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{p}(\alpha u+\beta v)^{i}v^{r-i}\bigl(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{r-i}{p-i}\bigr)\beta^{p-i}\alpha^{r+1-p}\end{array}

for each
p=0,\ldots,r-1
.
Comparing the coefficients of
u^{k}v^{r-k}
, we get

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\bigl(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{r+1}{p-k}\bigr)\beta^{p-k}\alpha^{r+1-(p-k)}&=&\sumop\displaylimits_{i=k}^{p}\binom{i}{k}\beta^{i-k}\alpha^{k}\bigl(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{r-i}{p-i}\bigr)\beta^{p-i}\alpha^{r+1-p},\end{array}

so the result follows from the next combinatoric lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.4.4
.

For all integers
0\leq k\leq p\leq r
, we have

\binom{r+1}{p-k}=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=k}^{p}\binom{i}{i-k}\binom{r-i}{p-i}.

Proof.

The sides of the equations count the number of elements of the sets

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{S}&=&\vphantom{\Big)}\{C\subset\{0,\ldots,r\}\mid C\mbox{ contains }p-k\mbox{ elements}\},\\
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}&=&\left\{(i,A,B)\left|\begin{array}[]{ll}i\in\{k,\ldots,p\},A\subset\{0,\ldots,i-1\},B\subset\{i+1,\ldots,r\},\\
A\mbox{ contains }i-k\mbox{ elements},B\mbox{ contains }p-i\mbox{ elements}\end{array}\right\},\right.\end{array}

so it remains to prove that the map
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{S}
,
(i,A,B)\mapsto A\cup B
is bijective. This corresponds to showing that for each
C\in\mathcal{S}
, there exists a unique
i\in\{k,\ldots,p\}\setminus C
such that
C\cap\{0,\ldots,i-1\}
contains
i-k
elements. This is because the map

\tau\colon\{k,\ldots,p\}\to\mathbb{N},\quad i\mapsto(i-k)-\lvert C\cap\{0,\ldots,i-1\}\rvert

is non-decreasing, is increasing outside
C
, and satisfies
\tau(k)\leq 0\leq\tau(p)
.
∎

Lemma 3.4.5
.

Let
a\geq 0,b\geq 1,c\geq 2
. Let
\pi\colon Z_{a}^{b,c,P}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
be the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle induced by
P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}P_{i}(z)z^{ai+1}
with
P_{i}\in\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai}

(
Notation
\ref{Not:ZabcP})
.

For each
\varphi\in\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
, the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\varphi\pi
is isomorphic to
Z_{a}^{b,c,\hat{P}}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
, where
\hat{P}
is defined by

\hat{P}(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}\hat{P}_{i}(z)z^{ai+1}
, with
\hat{P}_{i}\in\mathrm{k}[z,z^{-1}]
given as follows:

(1)

If
\varphi([y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}])=[y_{0}:y_{1};\alpha z_{0}+\beta z_{1}:\gamma z_{0}+\delta z_{1}]
for some
\sigma=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
\gamma&\delta\end{bmatrix}\in\GL_{2}
, then
\hat{P}_{i}=\sigma(P_{i})
for each
i
, where the action of
\,\GL_{2}
on the vector space
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-ai}
is that of Definition
3.4.1
.

(2)

If
a=0
and
\varphi([y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}])=[\alpha y_{0}+\beta y_{1};\gamma y_{0}+\delta y_{1}:z_{0}:z_{1}]
for some
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\in\mathrm{k}
with
\alpha\delta-\beta\gamma\neq 0
, then
\hat{P}(y_{0},y_{1},z)
satisfies

P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\hat{P}(\alpha y_{0}+\beta y_{1},\gamma y_{0}+\delta y_{1},z)
.

(3)

If
a\geq 1
and
\varphi([y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}])=[y_{0}:y_{1}+y_{0}R(z_{0},z_{1}):z_{0}:z_{1}]
for some
R\in\mathrm{k}[z_{0},z_{1}]_{a}
, then
\hat{P}
is such that
P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\hat{P}(y_{0},y_{1}+y_{0}R(z,1),z)
.

Proof.

Recall that the transition function is given by
\nu_{c,P}\in\Aut(\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
,

\begin{array}[]{c}\nu_{c,P}\colon\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)\mapsto\left([x_{0}:x_{1}z^{c}+x_{0}P(y_{0},y_{1},z);y_{0}z^{a}:y_{1}],\frac{1}{z}\right)\end{array}

and that the morphism
\pi\colon Z_{a}^{b,c,P}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
is given on the two charts by
\tau_{0},\tau_{1}\colon\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
, which send
([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)
onto, respectively,
[y_{0}:y_{1};1:z]
and
[y_{0}:y_{1};z:1]
.

We construct a transition function
\nu_{c,P^{\prime}}
of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\varphi\pi\colon X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
for some
\varphi\in\Aut(\mathbb{F}_{a})
and show that it is equivalent to that of
\hat{P}
(modulo the equivalence described in Lemma
3.3.6
). To do this, we find corresponding birational maps
\theta_{0}
and
\theta_{1}
compatible with the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{c,P}}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{c,P^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{a}}

(1)

When
\varphi([y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}])=[y_{0}:y_{1};\alpha z_{0}+\beta z_{1}:\gamma z_{0}+\delta z_{1}]
for some
\sigma=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
\gamma&\delta\end{bmatrix}\in\GL_{2}
, the action of
\varphi
on
\mathbb{F}_{a}
corresponds to

\begin{array}[]{llll}\ [y_{0}:y_{1};1:z]&\mapsto&[y_{0}:y_{1};\alpha+\beta z:\gamma+\delta z]=\big[(\alpha+\beta z)^{a}y_{0}:y_{1};1:\frac{\gamma+\delta z}{\alpha+\beta z}\big],\\
\ [y_{0}:y_{1};z:1]&\mapsto&[y_{0}:y_{1};\alpha z+\beta:\gamma z+\delta]=\big[(\gamma z+\delta)^{a}y_{0}:y_{1};\frac{\alpha z+\beta}{\gamma z+\delta}:1\big]\end{array}

on the two charts. To check that the action we gave on the moduli space is the right one, we only need to check it for generators of
\GL_{2}
.

(i)
We first do the case where
\gamma=0
(upper-triangular matrices). The second chart of
\mathbb{F}_{a}
is preserved, and
\varphi
corresponds to

\begin{array}[]{llllll}\ [y_{0}:y_{1};1:z]&\mapsto&[y_{0}:y_{1};\alpha+\beta z:\delta z]&=&\big[(\alpha+\beta z)^{a}y_{0}:y_{1};1:\frac{\delta z}{\alpha+\beta z}\big],\\
\ [y_{0}:y_{1};z:1]&\mapsto&[y_{0}:y_{1};\alpha z+\beta:\delta]&=&\big[\delta^{a}y_{0}:y_{1};\frac{\alpha z+\beta}{\delta}:1\big]\end{array}

on the two charts. It then suffices to choose

\begin{array}[]{rccc}\theta_{1}\colon&\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}&\xrightarrow{\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\simeq$}\vskip-2.0pt}}&\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\\
&([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)&\mapsto&\big([x_{0}:x_{1}:\delta^{a}y_{0}:y_{1}],\frac{\alpha z+\beta}{\delta}\big)\end{array}

and to choose a transition function
P^{\prime}(y_{0},y_{1},z)\in\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1},z]
such that
\theta_{0}=(\nu_{c,P^{\prime}})^{-1}\theta_{1}\nu_{c,P}
is a local isomorphism at each point where
z=0
. We compute that
\theta_{0}([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)
is equal to

\left(\left[x_{0}:\left(\frac{\beta z+\alpha}{\delta}\right)^{c}\left(x_{1}+\frac{x_{0}}{z^{c}}R(y_{0},y_{1},z)\right):(\beta z+\alpha)^{a}y_{0}:y_{1}\right],\frac{\delta z}{\beta z+\alpha}\right),

where
R(y_{0},y_{1},z)=P(y_{0},y_{1},z)-P^{\prime}\big(y_{0}(\beta z+\alpha)^{a},y_{1},\frac{\delta z}{\beta z+\alpha}\big)
. We then only need to choose the transition function
P^{\prime}(y_{0},y_{1},z)\in\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1},z]
so that
\theta_{0}
is a local isomorphism at each point where
z=0
, which corresponds to saying that the valuation of
z
at
R
is at least
c
.
We will observe that such a
P^{\prime}
exists in the equivalence class of
\hat{P}
. Writing
P^{\prime}(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i_{0}}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}P^{\prime}_{i}(z)z^{ai+1}
, we get

\begin{array}[]{rcl}P^{\prime}(y_{0}\big(\beta z+\alpha)^{a},y_{1},\frac{\delta z}{\beta z+\alpha}\big)&=&\sumop\displaylimits\limits_{i=0}^{b}{y_{0}}^{i}{y_{1}}^{b-i}\frac{\delta^{ai+1}P^{\prime}_{i}\left(\frac{\delta z}{\beta z+\alpha}\right)}{\beta z+\alpha}z^{ai+1},\end{array}

and then we need to choose the
P^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\frac{\delta^{ai+1}P^{\prime}_{i}({\delta z}/({\beta z+\alpha}))}{\beta z+\alpha}-P_{i}(z)
has valuation at least
c
at
z
. Since
\beta z+\alpha
is invertible in
\mathrm{k}[z]/(z^{c-ai-1})
, we find a unique solution in
\mathrm{k}[z]/(z^{c-ai-1})
, equal to
\frac{\alpha}{\delta^{c-1}}\sigma(P_{i})
by Lemma
3.4.3
(2)
.

(ii)
It remains to consider the case where
\sigma=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}
. The two charts are exchanged here, so it suffices to choose
\nu_{c,P^{\prime}}=(\nu_{c,P})^{-1}
, which yields
P^{\prime}(y_{0},y_{1},z)=-P(y_{0}z^{a},y_{1},z^{-1})z^{c}
, which is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{rcl}P\big(y_{0}z^{a},y_{1},\frac{1}{z}\big)z^{c}&=&\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}(y_{0}z^{a})^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}P_{i}\big(\frac{1}{z}\big)\big(\frac{1}{z}\big)^{ai+1}z^{c}\\[4.30554pt]
&=&\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}\bigl(P_{i}\big(\frac{1}{z}\big)\,z^{c-ai-2}\bigr)\,z^{ai+1}\\[4.30554pt]
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma}\penalty\ \ref{Lemm:TechnicalAction}\ref{DescInv}}}{{=}}&\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}\sigma(P_{i})\,z^{ai+1}.\end{array}

(2)

Now suppose
a=0
and that
\varphi([y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}])=[\alpha y_{0}+\beta y_{1};\gamma y_{0}+\delta y_{1}:z_{0}:z_{1}]
. Choosing
\theta_{1}=\theta_{2}\colon([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)\mapsto([x_{0}:x_{1}:\alpha y_{0}+\beta y_{1}:\gamma y_{0}+\delta y_{1}],z)
, we get
\nu_{c,\hat{P}}\theta_{1}=\theta_{2}\nu_{c,P}
with
P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\hat{P}(\alpha y_{0}+\beta y_{1},\gamma y_{0}+\delta y_{1},z)
.

(3)

Now suppose
a>0
and
\varphi([y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}])=[y_{0}:y_{1}+y_{0}R(z_{0},z_{1}):z_{0}:z_{1}]
for some
R\in\mathrm{k}[z_{0},z_{1}]_{a}
. We then choose
\theta_{1}\colon([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)\mapsto([x_{0}:x_{1}:y_{0}:y_{1}+y_{0}R(z,1)],z)
and
\theta_{2}\colon([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)\mapsto

([x_{0}:x_{1}:y_{0}:y_{1}+y_{0}R(1,z)],z)
and get
\nu_{c,\hat{P}}\theta_{1}=\theta_{2}\nu_{c,P}
with
P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\hat{P}(y_{0},y_{1}+y_{0}R(z,1),z)
.
∎

Corollary 3.4.6
.

Let
a,b,c\in\mathbb{Z}
, with
a\geq 0
,
b\geq 1
, and
c\geq 2
. We recall that
\GL_{2}
acts on
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq r}

(
see Definition
\ref{GL2 action})
and on
\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b}
when
a=0

(
see Lemma
\ref{Lemm:ActionAutFaParSpace}\ref{ActionGL20})
.
We have the following equivariant isomorphisms:

(1)

If
a\geq 1
, then
\mathcal{M}_{a}^{b,c}\simeq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigoplusop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}\cdot k[z]_{\leq c-2-ai}\right)
as
\GL_{2}
-varieties.

(2)

If
a=0
, then as
\GL_{2}\times\GL_{2}
-varieties,

\mathcal{M}_{0}^{b,c}\simeq\mathbb{P}\big(k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b}\otimes k[z]_{\leq c-2}\big)=\mathbb{P}\big(\Hom((k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b})^{*},k[z]_{\leq c-2})\big).

Moreover, if
b=c-2
, then
\Hom^{\GL_{2}}((k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b})^{*},k[z]_{\leq b})=\{\lambda I;\lambda\in k\}
, and the identity element corresponds to
Z_{a}^{b,b+2,P}
, with

P=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}P_{i}(z)z\;

and
\;P_{i}(z)=z^{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,b
.

Proof.

Assertion
(1)
and the first part of
(2)
follow from Corollary
3.3.8
and Lemma
3.4.5
.

We now assume
b=c-2
and define a non-degenerate bilinear form
\phi
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\phi:&k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b}&\times&k[z]_{\leq b}&\to&k\\
&\big(\sumop\displaylimits_{j=0}^{b}c_{j}y_{0}^{j}y_{1}^{b-j}&,&\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}d_{i}z^{i}\big)&\mapsto&\sumop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{b}c_{l}d_{l}.\\
\end{array}

The map
\phi
is
\GL_{2}
-invariant. Indeed, by bilinearity, it suffices to check that for all
g\in\GL_{2}
, for all
j=0,\ldots,b
, and for all
(u,v)\in k^{2}
, we have

\phi\left(g\cdot y_{0}^{j}y_{1}^{b-j},g\cdot\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}u^{i}v^{b-i}z^{i}\right)=\phi\left(y_{0}^{j}y_{1}^{b-j},\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}u^{i}v^{b-i}z^{i}\right)=u^{j}v^{b-j}.

This can be checked directly for
g=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}
and
g=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
0&\delta\end{bmatrix}
. Since these elements generate
\GL_{2}
, the
\GL_{2}
-invariance of
\phi
follows.

Therefore, the
\GL_{2}
-representations
\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b}
and
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq b}
are dual to each other. Since they are both irreducible (a consequence of Remark
3.4.2
), it follows from Schur’s lemma that
\Hom^{\GL_{2}}((k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b})^{*},k[z]_{\leq b})=\{\lambda I;\lambda\in k\}
. Identifying
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq b}
with
(\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b})^{*}
via the map
\phi
above, we see that
\{1,z,\ldots,z^{b}\}
is the dual basis of
\{y_{1}^{b},y_{0}y_{1}^{b-1},\ldots,y_{0}^{b}\}
. Hence the identity element corresponds to
\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}z^{i}
as an element of
\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b}\otimes k[z]_{\leq b}
, and so it corresponds to the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
Z_{a}^{b,b+2,P}
, with
P
as in the statement of the corollary.
∎

Remark 3.4.7
.

In the proof of Corollary
3.4.6
, we use the fact that the ground field is of characteristic zero to say that the
\GL_{2}
-representations
\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b}
and
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq b}
are irreducible (this is used to prove the last part of the statement). The statement of the corollary is true over any algebraically closed field of arbitrary characteristic, but a general proof is slightly more complicated since we can no longer invoke Schur’s lemma to prove that
\Hom^{\GL_{2}}((k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b})^{*},k[z]_{\leq b})=\{\lambda I;\lambda\in k\}
, and we have to do the calculation by hand (using Lemma
3.4.5
).

3.5. The
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-bundles
\boldsymbol{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}

We now study two families of non-decomposable
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
, in §
3.5
and §
3.6
, which will play an important role further on (see Proposition
3.7.4
).

Definition 3.5.1
.

For each integer
b\geq 1
, we define
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{F}_{0}=\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
to be the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
Z_{0}^{b,b+2,P}\to\mathbb{F}_{0}
, where
P=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}P_{i}(z)z

and
P_{i}(z)=z^{i}
.

Remark 3.5.2
.

The
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{F}_{0}
naturally arises in Corollary
3.4.6
(2)
, which explains why the image of
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})\to\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{0})
contains the diagonal group
H_{\Delta}=\{(g,g)\mid g\in\PGL_{2}\}\subset\PGL_{2}\times\PGL_{2}=\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{0})
. Lemma
3.5.5
below makes this explicit and shows that
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})\simeq\PGL_{2}
.

Remark 3.5.3
.

The
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{F}_{0}
has numerical invariants
(0,b,b+2)
since it is equal to
Z_{0}^{b,b+2,P}
for some polynomial
P
(see Remark
3.3.5
).

Remark 3.5.4
.

We will prove in Lemma
4.2.4
that the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
of Definition
3.5.1
coincides with the lift of the Schwarzenberger bundle
\mathcal{S}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
of Definition
1.2.7
.

Lemma 3.5.5
.

Let
b\geq 1
be an integer, and let us denote by
\pi,\pi^{\prime}
the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles
\pi\colon\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\pi^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{F}_{0}^{b+1,b+1}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Then, the following hold:

(1)

For each
i=1,2
, denoting by
\mathrm{pr}_{i}\colon\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
the
i
-th projection, the morphism
\mathrm{pr}_{i}\pi\colon\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is a
\mathbb{F}_{b}
-bundle.
Denoting by
S_{i}\subset\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
the union of the
(-b)
-curves of the
\mathbb{F}_{b}
, the intersection
C=S_{1}\cap S_{2}
is a curve isomorphic to the diagonal
\Delta\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
via
\pi
. It corresponds to the intersection of
\pi^{-1}(\Delta)
with the surface
x_{0}=0
in both charts.

(2)

We have
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})\simeq\PGL_{2}
and a commutative diagram

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon}
\textstyle{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{0}^{b+1,b+1},\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}

where all maps are
\PGL_{2}
-equivariant, the action of
\PGL_{2}
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is the diagonal one, the action of
\PGL_{2}
on
\mathcal{F}_{0}^{b+1,b+1}
is given by

(3.2)

[x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}]\mapsto[x_{0}:x_{1};\alpha y_{0}+\beta y_{1}:\gamma y_{0}+\delta y_{1};\alpha z_{0}+\beta z_{1}:\gamma z_{0}+\delta z_{1}],

the morphism
\epsilon
is the blow-up of the curve
C
, and the morphism
\eta
is the blow-up of the curve
C^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{F}_{0}^{b+1,b+1}
given by
\mathbb{P}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{0}^{b+1,b+1}
,
[u:v]\mapsto[1:1:u:v:u:v]
.

Moreover, every automorphism of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is trivial.

(3)

If
\mathrm{char}(\mathrm{k})
does not divide
b+1
, the curve
C
is the unique curve invariant by
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})
.

Proof.

We write
m=b+1
. The fact that (
3.2
) yields an action of
\PGL_{2}
on
\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}
follows from the fact that

[x_{0}:x_{1};\lambda y_{0}:\lambda y_{1};\lambda z_{0}:\lambda z_{1}]=[\lambda^{m}x_{0}:\lambda^{m}x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}]=[x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}]
for each
\lambda\in\mathrm{k}^{*}
. The same argument shows that the map
\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}
,
[u:v]\mapsto[1:1:u:v:u:v]
is a well-defined closed embedding. The image
C^{\prime}
is sent to the diagonal
\Delta\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and the action on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
(via the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
) is the diagonal action.

We now construct a birational map
\varphi\colon\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}\dashrightarrow\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{m-1}=\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
.

The two open subsets
U_{0},U_{1}\subset\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}
where
z_{0}\neq 0
and
z_{1}\neq 0
, respectively, are isomorphic to
\mathbb{F}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
via

\begin{array}[]{rccc}&\mathbb{F}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}&\hookrightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}\\
\iota_{0}\colon&\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)&\mapsto&[x_{1}:x_{0};y_{0}:y_{1};1:z],\\
\iota_{1}\colon&\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)&\mapsto&[x_{1}:x_{0};y_{0}:y_{1};z:1].\end{array}

The transition function
\theta^{\prime}=(\iota_{1})^{-1}\iota_{0}\in\Bir(\mathbb{F}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
is then given by

\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)\mapsto\big([x_{0}:x_{1}z^{m};y_{0}:y_{1}],\frac{1}{z}\big),

and the curve
C^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}
yields a section of
\mathbb{F}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
, given by
\mathbb{A}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
,
z\mapsto([1:1;1:z],z)
and
z\mapsto([1:1;z:1],z)
, respectively, on the two charts. We can then blow up
C
and contract the strict transform of
{\pi^{\prime}}^{-1}(\pi^{\prime}(C^{\prime}))
; we do it on the two charts via

\begin{array}[]{rccc}&\mathbb{F}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}&\dashrightarrow&\mathbb{F}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\\
\varphi_{0}\colon&\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)&\mapsto&\big([x_{0}(y_{0}z-y_{1}):x_{1}-x_{0}y_{0}^{m};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big),\\
\varphi_{1}\colon&\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)&\mapsto&\big([x_{0}(y_{0}-y_{1}z):x_{1}-x_{0}y_{1}^{m};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big).\end{array}

Computing the transition function
\theta=\varphi_{1}\theta^{\prime}(\varphi_{0})^{-1}\in\Bir(\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
, we obtain

\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)\mapsto\left(\left[x_{0}:x_{1}z^{m+1}+x_{0}z\frac{y_{0}^{m}z^{m}-y_{1}^{m}}{y_{0}z-y_{1}};y_{0}:y_{1}\right],\frac{1}{z}\right),

which is the transition function of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
(see Definition
3.5.1
).

Since
C^{\prime}
and
\pi^{\prime-1}(\pi^{\prime}(C^{\prime}))=\pi^{\prime-1}(\Delta)
(where
\Delta\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is the diagonal) are invariant by
\PGL_{2}
, the birational map
\varphi
is
\PGL_{2}
-equivariant, for some biregular action of
\PGL_{2}
on
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
, acting diagonally on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and preserving the curve
C\subset\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
being the image of the contracted surface
\pi^{\prime-1}(\pi^{\prime}(C^{\prime}))
. Note that this curve is given by the intersection of
x_{0}=0
with
\pi^{-1}(\Delta)
in both charts, where
\Delta\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is the diagonal (this follows by replacing
y_{0}
and
y_{1}
by
1
and
z
in
\varphi_{0}
and by
z
and
1
in
\varphi_{1}
). This yields the commutative diagram of
(2)
, with
\psi=\varphi^{-1}
. To achieve the proof of
(2)
, we only need to show that the homomorphism
\PGL_{2}\to\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})
constructed by this map is surjective.

The second projection
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\colon\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
satisfies that
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\pi\colon\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is a
\mathbb{F}_{b}
-bundle, trivial on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus[0:1]
and
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus[1:0]
with transition function
\theta
. The union of the
(-b)
-curves of the
\mathbb{F}_{b}
is a surface
S_{2}\subset\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
, which corresponds to
x_{0}=0
on both charts and is then sent by
\psi
onto the surface
S_{2}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}
given by
x_{1}=0
.

The involution
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Aut(\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m})
given by
\sigma^{\prime}\colon[x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}]\mapsto[x_{1}:x_{0};z_{0}:z_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}]
commutes with
\PGL_{2}
and preserves
C^{\prime}
and
\pi^{\prime-1}(\pi^{\prime}(C))
; hence
\sigma=\psi^{-1}\sigma^{\prime}\psi=\varphi\sigma^{\prime}\varphi^{-1}\in\Aut(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})
. Since
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
act on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
by the exchange of the two factors,
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\pi\colon\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is also an
\mathbb{F}_{b}
-bundle. The union of the
(-b)
-curves of the
\mathbb{F}_{b}
is a surface
S_{1}=\sigma(S_{2})\subset\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
, which is then sent by
\psi
onto the surface
S_{1}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}
given by
x_{0}=0
. The two surface
S_{1}^{\prime},S_{2}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}
are disjoint and also disjoint from
C^{\prime}
. Their strict transforms on
X
are then again disjoint, and their images on
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
intersect only along
C
. This yields
(1)
.

To prove
(2)
, it remains to show that
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})\simeq\PGL_{2}
and that every automorphism of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is trivial. Every element of
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})
permutes the fibres of the two
\mathbb{F}_{b}
-bundles
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\pi,\mathrm{pr}_{2}\pi\colon\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, so
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are both invariant, and the same holds for
C=S_{1}\cap S_{2}
. Since
\pi(C)=\Delta\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, the image of
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})\to\Autz(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1})
is the diagonal
\PGL_{2}
. It then suffices to see that every automorphism of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is trivial. This amounts to showing that every automorphism of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
that fixes
C^{\prime}
is trivial. Indeed, every automorphism of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
preserves
S_{1}^{\prime}
and
S_{2}^{\prime}
, so is of the form

[x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}]\mapsto[\lambda x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}]

for some
\lambda\in\mathrm{k}^{*}
. It preserves
C^{\prime}
if and only if
\lambda=1
.

We finish the proof by proving
(3)
. It follows from the construction that
C=S_{1}\cap S_{2}\subset\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
is invariant by
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})
. It remains to show that every curve
\ell\subset\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
invariant by
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})
is equal to
C
when
\mathrm{char}(\mathrm{k})
does not divide
b+1
. The action of
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
being the diagonal action of
\PGL_{2}
, we find that
\pi(\ell)
is the diagonal
\Delta
and thus have to see that
C
is the only curve invariant by the action of
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})\simeq\PGL_{2}
on the surface
V=\pi^{-1}(\Delta)
, which is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
V\to\Delta
. To do this, it suffices to find an isomorphism
V\xrightarrow{\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\simeq$}\vskip-2.0pt}}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
that sends
C
onto the diagonal. Indeed, the action of
\PGL_{2}
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
will then have to be the diagonal action, which only preserves the diagonal.

We restrict the transition function of
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
to
V
and get

\big([x_{0}:x_{1};1:z],z\big)\mapsto\left([x_{0}:x_{1}z^{b+2}+x_{0}(b+1)z^{b+1};1:z],\frac{1}{z}\right)=\left([x_{0}:x_{1}z^{2}+x_{0}(b+1)z;\frac{1}{z}:1],\frac{1}{z}\right).

The curve
C
corresponds to
x_{0}=0
on both charts. The isomorphism
V\xrightarrow{\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\simeq$}\vskip-2.0pt}}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
can then be chosen on the two charts as

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\big([x_{0}:x_{1};1:z],z\big)&\mapsto&\big([x_{1}:x_{0}(b+1)+x_{1}z],[1:z]\big),\\
\big([x_{0}:x_{1};z:1],z\big)&\mapsto&\big([-(b+1)x_{0}+x_{1}z:x_{1}],[z:1]\big),\end{array}

which is an isomorphism since
b+1\neq 0
(as we assumed that
\mathrm{char}(\mathrm{k})
does not divide
b+1
).
∎

Remark 3.5.6
.

When
\mathrm{char}(\mathrm{k})
divides
b+1
, there are actually two curves invariant by
\Autz(\hat{S}_{b})\simeq\PGL_{2}
.

Remark 3.5.7
.

Let
m=b+1
. An element
g=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
\gamma&\delta\end{bmatrix}\in\PGL_{2}
sends
p_{0}=[1:1;1:0;0:1]\in\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}
to
[1:1;\alpha:\gamma;\beta:\delta]
. Therefore,
g\cdot p_{0}=p_{0}
if and only if
\beta=\gamma=0
and
\alpha^{m}=\delta^{m}=1
, and so
H:=\mathrm{Stab}_{p_{0}}\PGL_{2}=\left\{\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&0\\
0&\alpha^{-1}\end{bmatrix};\ \alpha^{m}=1\right\}
. As
\dim(\PGL_{2}/H)=3
, we see that
\PGL_{2}/H
is a dense open orbit for the diagonal action of
\PGL_{2}
on
\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}
. As
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
is
\PGL_{2}
-equivariantly birational to
\mathcal{F}_{0}^{m,m}
, the same holds for
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
.

3.6. Umemura
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundles

In this section we introduce a new class of non-decomposable
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles on Hirzebruch surfaces. To the best of the authors’ knowledge, those appeared for the first time in the work of Umemura
[
Ume88
, § 10]
, and that is the reason why we chose to call them Umemura bundles.

Definition 3.6.1

(Umemura bundles)
.

Let
a,b\geq 1
and
c\geq 2
be such that
c=ak+2
with
0\leq k\leq b
. We
call
Umemura
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}

given by
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}=Z_{a}^{b,c,P}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
with
P=y_{0}^{k}y_{1}^{b-k}z^{c-1}
.

Remark 3.6.2
.

Recall (see Notation
3.3.4
) that
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}=Z_{a}^{b,c,P}
is
obtained by the gluing of two copies of
\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
along
\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}
by the automorphism
\nu\in\Aut(\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
,

\begin{array}[]{ccl}\nu\colon([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)&\mapsto&\left([x_{0}:x_{1}z^{c}+x_{0}y_{0}^{k}y_{1}^{b-k}z^{c-1};y_{0}z^{a}:y_{1}],\frac{1}{z}\right)\\
&=&\left([x_{0}:x_{1}z^{c-ab}+x_{0}y_{0}^{k}y_{1}^{b-k}z^{c-ab-1};y_{0}:y_{1}z^{-a}],\frac{1}{z}\right),\end{array}

and that
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
sends
([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)\in\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
onto, respectively,
[y_{0}:y_{1};1:z]\in\mathbb{F}_{a}
and
[y_{0}:y_{1};z:1]\in\mathbb{F}_{a}
on the two charts. It then has numerical invariants
(a,b,c)
.

Lemma 3.6.3
.

Let
a,b\geq 1
and
c\geq 2
be such that
c=ak+2
with
0\leq k\leq b
. The morphism
\pi\colon\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
yields a surjective group homomorphism

\rho\colon\Autz(\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c})\twoheadrightarrow\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a}).

Proof.

The statement corresponds to showing that the element of
\mathcal{M}_{a}^{b,c}
corresponding to
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}
is fixed by the whole group
\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
. We only need to check it for generators of
\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
, using Lemma
3.4.5
.

Recall that the polynomial
P
is given by
P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}P_{i}(z)z^{ai+1}
, with
P_{i}\in\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai}
(see Notation
3.3.4
), where all
P_{i}
are zero except one, namely
P_{k}
, which is equal to
1
. Lemma
3.4.5
(1)
shows that
\GL_{2}
fixes the class of
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}
in
\mathcal{M}_{a}^{b,c}
since
P_{k}
is sent onto
\sigma(P_{k})\in\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ak}=\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq 0}=\mathrm{k}
. Lemma
3.4.5
(3)
then shows that an automorphism
[y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}])=[y_{0}:y_{1}+y_{0}R(z_{0},z_{1}):z_{0}:z_{1}]
of
\mathbb{F}_{a}
sends
P
onto the polynomial
P^{\prime}
equivalent to the polynomial
\hat{P}(y_{0},y_{1},z)
that satisfies
y_{0}^{k}y_{1}^{b-k}z^{c-1}=P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\hat{P}(y_{0},y_{1}+y_{0}R(z,1),z)
. The polynomial
\hat{P}_{k}
is then equal to
P_{k}=1
, and all the polynomials
\hat{P}_{i}
with
i<k
are zero and thus equal to
P_{i}
. The other coefficients do not appear in the equivalence class since
c-ai-2<0
for these.
∎

Remark 3.6.4
.

With the notation above, the group of automorphisms of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles of
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}
identifies with the vector group
\bigoplusop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{b}k[z_{0},z_{1}]_{ai-c}
whose action on
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}
can be described on the first chart by the following biregular action:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}&\to&\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\\
\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)&\mapsto&\left([x_{0}:x_{1}+x_{0}\sumop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}q_{i}(z,1);y_{0}:y_{1}],z\right),\end{array}

where
Q=(q_{i})_{i=1,\ldots,b}\in\bigoplusop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{b}k[z_{0},z_{1}]_{ai-c}
.
This can be computed for instance by using the transition function of
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}
.
The action on
\pi^{-1}(s_{-a})
, which corresponds to
y_{0}=0
on both charts, is then trivial.

Remark 3.6.5
.

The group
\GL_{2}
acts on
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}
by acting rationally on both charts via, respectively,

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}&\hskip-5.69046pt\to&\hskip-8.5359pt\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\\
([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)&\hskip-5.69046pt\mapsto&\hskip-8.5359pt\left([x_{0}:x_{1}\frac{(\beta z+\alpha)^{c}}{\alpha\delta-\beta\gamma}\!+\!x_{0}\frac{\beta(\beta z+\alpha)^{c-1}y_{0}^{k}y_{1}^{b-k}}{\alpha\delta-\beta\gamma};y_{0}(\beta z+\alpha)^{a};y_{1}],\frac{\delta z+\gamma}{\beta z+\alpha}\right),\\[4.30554pt]
([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)&\hskip-5.69046pt\mapsto&\hskip-8.5359pt\left([x_{0}:x_{1}\frac{(\gamma z+\delta)^{c}}{\alpha\delta-\beta\gamma}\!-\!x_{0}\frac{\gamma(\gamma z+\delta)^{c-1}y_{0}^{k}y_{1}^{b-k}}{\alpha\delta-\beta\gamma};y_{0}(\gamma z+\delta)^{a};y_{1}],\frac{\alpha z+\beta}{\gamma z+\delta}\right)\end{array}

as can directly be checked using the transition function.

3.7. Invariant fibres

The following result shows that one can reduce the study of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
to the case where the action of
\Autz(X)
on
\mathbb{F}_{a}
is transitive on the set on fibres of
\tau_{a}\colon\mathbb{F}_{a}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
. This is in particular the case when the action on
\mathbb{F}_{a}
yields a surjective group homomorphism
\Autz(X)\twoheadrightarrow\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
(see Remark
2.4.5
) and holds for decomposable
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles (see Lemma
3.1.5
), for the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
(see Lemma
3.5.5
), and for Umemura bundles (see Lemma
3.6.3
).

Lemma 3.7.1
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
be a
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle. If there is a point
p\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
such that the surface
\tau\pi^{-1}(p)\subset X
is invariant by
\Autz(X)

(
where
\tau_{a}\colon\mathbb{F}_{a}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is the standard
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
)
, then there exist a decomposable bundle
\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
and a commutative diagram

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{a}\hbox to0.0pt{\,,\hss}}

where
\psi
is a birational map satisfying
\psi\Autz(X)\psi^{-1}\subsetneq\Autz(\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c})
.

Proof.

The surface
S=\mathbb{F}_{a}\setminus(\tau_{a})^{-1}(p)
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
, and
U=\pi^{-1}(S)
is invariant by
\Autz(X)
. Applying Proposition
3.2.2
, one can perform finitely many
\Autz(X)
-equivariant birational maps and reduce to the case where
\tau_{a}\pi\colon U\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\{b\}
is a trivial
\mathbb{F}_{b}
-bundle. We then find an integer
c\geq 0
and an inclusion
U\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}
such that the action of
\Autz(X)
extends to a biregular action on
\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}
. This will yield a birational map as above, satisfying
\psi\Autz(X)\psi^{-1}\subset\Autz(\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c})
. Moreover, equality does not hold since
\Autz(\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c})
acts transitively on the set of fibres of
\tau_{a}\colon\mathbb{F}_{a}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
(see Lemma
3.1.5
and Remark
2.4.5
).

If
b>0
, the action of every element
g\in\Autz(X)
on
U
corresponds to an automorphism of
\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
of the form

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)&\mapsto&\big([x_{0}:\alpha x_{1}+x_{0}\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}p_{i}(z);f_{1}(y_{0},y_{1},z),f_{2}(y_{0},y_{1},z)],az+b\big)\end{array}

for some
\alpha\in\mathrm{k}^{*}
and
p_{i}\in\mathrm{k}[z]
, where
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1},z]
correspond to the coordinates of the restriction of an automorphism of
\mathbb{F}_{a}
. The group
\Autz(X)
being an algebraic group, the degrees of the polynomials
p_{i}
are bounded by an integer which does not depend on
g\in\Autz(X)
. It then suffices to take an integer
c>0
big enough and to send
([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)\in\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
to
[x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1};z:1]\in\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}
to be able to extend the action of all elements
g\in\Autz(X)
to
\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}
. (This can be checked for instance using the description of
\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}
provided in §
3
.)

If
b=0
, the action of every element
g\in\Autz(X)
on
U
corresponds similarly to an automorphism of
\mathbb{F}_{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}=\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
of the form

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)&\mapsto&\big([\alpha(z)x_{0}+\beta(z)x_{1}:\gamma(z)x_{0}+\delta(z)x_{1};f_{1}(y_{0},y_{1},z),f_{2}(y_{0},y_{1},z)],az+b\big),\end{array}

where
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\in\mathrm{k}[z]
are such that
\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
\gamma&\delta\end{bmatrix}\in\PGL_{2}(\mathrm{k}[z])

(
i.e.

\alpha\delta-\beta\gamma\in\mathrm{k}^{*})
and where
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1},z]
correspond to the coordinates of the restriction of an automorphism of
\mathbb{F}_{a}
.

The morphism
U\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
,
([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z)\mapsto([x_{0}:x_{1}],z)
then yields an algebraic group homomorphism
\Autz(X)\to\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
. The image
H\subset\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
is then a connected algebraic subgroup of
\Bir(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
that preserves the set of fibres
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
. There thus exist an integer
c\geq 0
and an inclusion
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{F}_{c}
which allows the image to extend. We then find an inclusion
U\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}
which allows the action of
\Autz(X)
to extend.∎

Lemma 3.7.2
.

Let
H
be a maximal proper connected subgroup of
\PGL_{2}\times\PGL_{2}
. Then
H
is
B\times\PGL_{2}
or
\PGL_{2}\times B
, where
B\subset\PGL_{2}
is a Borel subgroup
(
conjugate to the group of upper-triangular matrices
)
, or
H
is isomorphic to
\PGL_{2}
. In the latter case,
H
is conjugate to the diagonal embedding of
\,\PGL_{2}
in
\PGL_{2}\times\PGL_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
be the two natural projections
\PGL_{2}\times\PGL_{2}\to\PGL_{2}
. If
p_{i}(H)\subsetneq\PGL_{2}
, then
p_{i}(H)
is contained in a Borel subgroup
B
of
\PGL_{2}
(
e.g.
by
[
Hum75
, § 30.4, Theorem (a)]
), and so
H
is
B\times\PGL_{2}
or
\PGL_{2}\times B
since
H
is maximal.

We now assume
p_{i}(H)=\PGL_{2}
for
i=1,2
. Let
K
be the kernel of
{p_{1}}_{|H}:\;H\to\PGL_{2}
. As
K
is a normal subgroup of
H
and
{p_{2}}_{|H}
is onto,
p_{2}(K)
is a normal subgroup of
\PGL_{2}
. As
H
is a proper subgroup of
\PGL_{2}\times\PGL_{2}
, we must have
p_{2}(K)=\{1\}
, and so
K=\{1\}
and
{p_{1}}_{|H}
is a bijective morphism of algebraic groups
H\to\PGL_{2}
. In particular,
\dim(H)=3
by
[
Hum75
, § 4.1, Theorem]
.

We now show that
H
is simple. Let
N
be a normal subgroup of
H
. As
{p_{i}}_{|H}
is onto,
p_{i}(N)
is a normal subgroup of
\PGL_{2}
. If
p_{i}(N)=\PGL_{2}
for some
i
, then
N=H
(as they have the same dimension). Otherwise,
p_{1}(N)=p_{2}(N)=\{1\}
, and so
N=\{1\}
. This achieves to prove that
H
is a simple group.

As
\dim(H)=3
and
H
is of rank at most
2
, the classification of simple root systems yields that
H
is isomorphic to
\SL_{2}
or
\PGL_{2}
; see
[
Hum75
, § 32 and Appendix]
. Since
{p_{i}}_{|H}:H\to\PGL_{2}
is a bijective morphism,
H
cannot be isomorphic to
\SL_{2}
(otherwise
\pm I_{2}
would be sent to
I_{2}
), and so
H\simeq\PGL_{2}
. In this case,
H
is conjugate to the diagonal embedding of
\PGL_{2}
in
\PGL_{2}\times\PGL_{2}
. Indeed, this follows from the fact that a bijective morphism of algebraic groups is an isomorphism in characteristic zero (
e.g.
by Zariski’s main theorem), together with the fact that all automorphisms of
\PGL_{2}
are inner
[
Dem65
, Theorem 1.3 and § 3.6]
.
∎

Remark 3.7.3
.

In characteristic
p>0
, the above result is false: we get infinitely many embeddings of
\PGL_{2}
into
\PGL_{2}\times\PGL_{2}
with pairwise distinct images, up to conjugation, given by
\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\mapsto\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}a^{p^{n}}&b^{p^{n}}\\
c^{p^{n}}&d^{p^{n}}\end{bmatrix}\right)
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proposition 3.7.4
.

Let
a\geq 0
, and let
\pi\colon X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
be a
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle.
Then, there exist
b,c\in\mathbb{Z}
such that one of the following holds:

(1)

X
is isomorphic to a decomposable
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\mathcal{F}^{b,c}_{a}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}

(
Definition
\ref{def Fabc})
;

(2)

X
is isomorphic to an Umemura
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\mathcal{U}_{a}^{b,c}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}

(
Definition
\ref{def Umemura bundle})
;

(3)

we have
a=0
, and
(X,\pi)
is square isomorphic to the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{F}_{0}
of Definition
3.5.1
; or

(4)

there exist a
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\tau\colon\mathbb{F}_{a}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
and a closed point
p\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
such that
(\tau\pi)^{-1}(p)
is invariant by
\Autz(X)
.

In cases
(3)
and
(4)
, there exist a decomposable bundle
\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
and a commutative diagram

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{a}\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

where
\psi
is a birational map satisfying
\psi\Autz(X)\psi^{-1}\subsetneq\Autz(\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c})
.

Proof.

Lemma
3.7.1
and Lemma
3.5.5

(2)
give the existence of the birational map
\psi\colon X\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c}
in cases
(3)
and
(4)
, with
\psi\Autz(X)\psi^{-1}\subset\Autz(\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c})
. Moreover, we cannot have equality since the action of
\Autz(\mathcal{F}_{a}^{b,c})\to\Aut(\mathbb{F}_{a})
is surjective (see Lemma
3.1.5
). It remains to show that we can reduce to the four cases above.

We denote by
H\subset\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
the image of
\Autz(X)
by the natural homomorphism
\Autz(X)\to\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
(see Lemma
2.1.1
). If the preimage of one fibre of
\tau_{a}\colon\mathbb{F}_{a}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is invariant by
\Autz(X)
, we get case
(4)
. We can in particular assume that all fibres of
\tau_{a}\pi\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
are isomorphic to
\mathbb{F}_{b}
for the same
b\geq 0
(no jumping fibre; see Proposition
3.2.2
). Hence,
X\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
has numerical invariants
(a,b,c)
for some
c\in\mathbb{Z}
, which is positive if
b=0
(see Proposition
3.3.1
and Definition
1.4.1
). We can moreover assume that
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
is not a decomposable bundle since otherwise we obtain case
(1)
. This implies that
b\geq 1
and
c\geq 2
and that
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
is isomorphic to

Z_{a}^{b,c,P}\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
, where
P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}{y_{0}}^{i}{y_{1}}^{b-i}P_{i}(z)z^{ai+1}
and
P_{i}(z)\in\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai}
for
i=0,\ldots,b
(see Corollary
3.3.7
).

For each
h\in\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{a})
, we have
h\in H
if and only if there exists an
\hat{h}\in\Autz(X)
such that the following diagram commutes:

(3.3)

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{h}}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{a}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

This is thus equivalent to asking that the class of
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
in
\mathcal{M}_{a}^{b,c}
(see Corollary
3.3.8
) is fixed by the action of
h
.

We now consider two cases, depending on whether
a\geq 1
or
a=0
.

Case
a\geq 1
. We denote by
i_{0}
the smallest integer such that
P_{i_{0}}\neq 0
. For each element
\overline{\sigma}\in\PGL_{2}
, there exists an element of
H\subseteq\Aut(\mathbb{F}_{a})
whose action on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, via
\tau
, corresponds to
\overline{\sigma}
in
\PGL_{2}
. We can write this element as
\varphi_{1}\varphi_{2}
, where
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in\Aut(\mathbb{F}_{a})
are given by

\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\varphi_{1}&\colon&[y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}]&\mapsto&[y_{0}:y_{1};\alpha z_{0}+\beta z_{1}:\gamma z_{0}+\delta z_{1}],\\[4.30554pt]
\varphi_{2}&\colon&[y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}]&\mapsto&[y_{0}:y_{1}+y_{0}R(z_{0},z_{1}):z_{0}:z_{1}],\end{array}

where
\sigma=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
\gamma&\delta\end{bmatrix}\in\GL_{2}
represents the class
\overline{\sigma}\in\PGL_{2}
and
R\in\mathrm{k}[z_{0},z_{1}]_{a}
. Lemma
3.4.5
describes the action of
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
on the class
[\pi]
. The element
\varphi_{2}
does not change the polynomial
P_{i_{0}}
(see Lemma
3.4.5
(3)
), so
\sigma(P_{i_{0}})
has to be equal to a multiple of
P_{i_{0}}
, for the action of
\GL_{2}
on
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-ai_{0}}
given in Definition
3.4.1
(see Lemma
3.4.5
(1)
). This implies that the class of
P_{i_{0}}
in
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai_{0}})
is fixed by the corresponding action of
\PGL_{2}
. This happens if and only if
(\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai_{0}})^{\SL_{2}}
is non-zero. As
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai_{0}}
is an irreducible
\SL_{2}
-representation (see Remark
3.4.2
),
(\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai_{0}})^{\SL_{2}}
is non-zero if and only if
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2-ai_{0}}
is the trivial representation; that is,
c-2=ai_{0}
and
P_{i_{0}}
is a constant polynomial. Since
i_{0}
is the smallest integer such that
P_{i_{0}}\neq 0
, we have
P_{i}=0
for all
i<i_{0}
. Moreover,
P_{i}=0
for
i>i_{0}
since
c-2-ai<0
. This implies that
P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\lambda y_{0}^{i_{0}}y_{1}^{b-i_{0}}z^{ai_{0}+1}
, and so that
X\to\mathbb{F}_{a}
is an Umemura bundle (see Definition
3.6.1
).

Case
a=0
. Let
a=0
and
\pi:X\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
be a
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle. If
H=\PGL_{2}\times\PGL_{2}=\Autz(\mathbb{F}_{0})
, then the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{0}^{b,c}
must contain a fixed point for the natural
H
-action, described in Corollary
3.4.6
(2)
. This cannot happen since the
\SL_{2}\times\SL_{2}
-representation
\mathrm{k}[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b}\otimes k[z]_{\leq c-2}
is irreducible, and non-trivial when
b\geq 1
(as we assumed above).

Now assume that
H\subsetneq\PGL_{2}\times\PGL_{2}
is a proper subgroup. If one of the two projections
H\to\PGL_{2}
is not onto, then one fibre of a projection
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is invariant, and we get case
(4)
. We then assume that
H
surjects onto
\PGL_{2}
via both projections. By Lemma
3.7.2
,
H
is conjugate to
H_{\Delta}:=\{(h,h)\ |\ h\in\PGL_{2}\}
in
\PGL_{2}\times\PGL_{2}
.

If
H
is conjugate to
H_{\Delta}
, then the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{0}^{b,c}
contains a fixed point for the natural
H_{\Delta}
-action. By Corollary
3.4.6
(2)
,
\mathcal{M}_{0}^{b,c}\simeq\mathbb{P}(\Hom((k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b})^{*},k[z]_{\leq c-2})
as an
H_{\Delta}
-variety, where we identify
H_{\Delta}=\PGL_{2}
. Hence
\mathcal{M}_{0}^{b,c}
contains a fixed point if and only if
\Hom^{\SL_{2}}((k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b})^{*},k[z]_{\leq c-2})\neq\{0\}
. As the
\SL_{2}
-representations
(k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b})^{*}
and
\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq c-2}
are irreducible and of dimension
b+1
and
c-1
, respectively, it follows from Schur’s lemma that
\Hom^{\SL_{2}}((k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b})^{*},k[z]_{\leq c-2})=0
when
b\neq c-2
. On the other hand, if
b=c-2
, then
\Hom^{\SL_{2}}((k[y_{0},y_{1}]_{b})^{*},k[z]_{\leq c-2})=\{\lambda Id;\lambda\in k\}
, and so
\mathcal{M}_{0}^{b,b+2}
has a unique fixed point corresponding to the identity; the latter is given by
P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}z^{i+1}
(see Corollary
3.4.6
(2)
) and yields case
(3)
.
∎

Remark 3.7.5
.

In the proof of Proposition
3.7.4
, the assumption that the base field
\mathrm{k}
is of characteristic zero is required. Indeed, the results from the representation theory of
\SL_{2}
that we use in the proof are not valid in positive characteristic. In positive characteristic, there are actually more
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles to consider.

4.
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-bundles over
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{P}^{2}}

The results in §
4.1
are valid over an algebraically closed field
\mathrm{k}
of arbitrary characteristic, but in §
4.2
we need to assume
\mathrm{char}(k)\neq 2
(due to the fact that we work with a quadratic form and need
2
to be invertible). In §
4.3
, Lemma
4.3.1
and Proposition
4.3.4
both rely on Proposition
3.7.4
, and so are valid only in characteristic zero, while Lemma
4.3.3
holds in characteristic different from
2
.

4.1. Decomposable bundles over
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{P}^{2}}

In this section, we give an explicit description of the decomposable
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles over
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, similar to the one provided in §
3.1
for the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles over the Hirzebruch surfaces. We also give global coordinates on decomposable
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles over
\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Definition 4.1.1
.

Let
b\in\mathbb{Z}
. Define
\mathcal{P}_{b}
to be the quotient of
(\mathbb{A}^{2}\setminus\{0\})\times(\mathbb{A}^{3}\setminus\{0\})
by the action of
(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}
given by

\begin{array}[]{ccc}(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}\times(\mathbb{A}^{2}\setminus\{0\})\times(\mathbb{A}^{3}\setminus\{0\})&\to&(\mathbb{A}^{2}\setminus\{0\})\times(\mathbb{A}^{3}\setminus\{0\})\\
\big((\mu,\rho),(y_{0},y_{1};z_{0},z_{1},z_{2})\big)&\mapsto&(\mu\rho^{-b}y_{0},\mu y_{1};\rho z_{0},\rho z_{1},\rho z_{2}).\end{array}

The class of
(y_{0},y_{1},z_{0},z_{1},z_{2})
will be written
[y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}:z_{2}]
. The projection

\mathcal{P}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{2},\quad[y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}:z_{2}]\mapsto[z_{0}:z_{1}:z_{2}]

identifies
\mathcal{P}_{b}
with

\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(b)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}\big)=\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-b)\big)

as a
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle over
\mathbb{P}^{2}
. As before, we get an isomorphism of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles
\mathcal{P}_{b}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{-b}
by exchanging
y_{0}
with
y_{1}
and will then often assume
b\geq 0
in the following.

Lemma 4.1.2
.

For each
b\in\mathbb{Z}
, the morphism
\pi\colon\mathcal{P}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
yields a surjective group homomorphism

\rho\colon\Autz(\mathcal{P}_{b})\twoheadrightarrow\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2})=\PGL_{3}.

Proof.

The existence of
\rho
is given by Lemma
2.1.1
. The fact that it is surjective can be seen by making
\GL_{3}
act naturally on
\mathcal{P}_{b}
, do nothing on
y_{0},y_{1}
, and act naturally on
z_{0},z_{1},z_{2}
.
∎

Remark 4.1.3
.

Assume
b\geq 1
. The group of automorphisms of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle of
\mathcal{P}_{b}
identifies with the (connected) group

\left\{\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
p&\lambda\end{bmatrix}\in\GL_{2}(\mathrm{k}[z_{0},z_{1},z_{2}])\ \middle|\ \lambda\in k^{*}\text{ and }p\in\mathrm{k}[z_{0},z_{1},z_{2}]_{b}\right\}

whose action on
\mathcal{P}_{b}
is as follows:

(\lambda,p)\cdot[y_{0}:y_{1};z_{0}:z_{1}:z_{2}]=[y_{0}:\lambda y_{1}+y_{0}p(z_{0},z_{1},z_{2});z_{0}:z_{1}:z_{2}].

This can be seen directly from the global description of
\mathcal{P}_{b}
in Definition
4.1.1
, and by using trivialisations on open subsets isomorphic to
\mathbb{A}^{2}
.

4.2. Schwarzenberger
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-bundles over
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{P}^{2}}

In this subsection, we study the Schwarzenberger
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles
\mathcal{S}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
, with
b\geq-1
, given by
\mathbb{P}(\kappa_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-b-1,0))\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
(see Definition
1.2.7
). As we will observe, only the cases
b\geq 1
are interesting since
\mathcal{S}_{-1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{0}
are decomposable (see Corollary
4.2.2
).

Lemma 4.2.1
.

Denoting by
U_{0},U_{1}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
the two open subsets

U_{0}=\{[X:Y:Z]|X\neq 0\}\simeq\mathbb{A}^{2},\quad U_{1}=\{[X:Y:Z]|Z\neq 0\}\simeq\mathbb{A}^{2},

the restriction of
\mathcal{S}_{b}
on
\mathbb{P}^{2}\setminus\{[0:1:0]\}
is obtained by gluing
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{0}
and
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{1}
along
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times(U_{0}\cap U_{1})
via the isomorphism given by

\begin{array}[]{rcccccc}\theta\colon&\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{0}&\dashrightarrow&\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{1}\\
&\left(\begin{bmatrix}x_{0}\\
x_{1}\end{bmatrix},[1:u:v]\right)&\mapsto&\left(\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{11}(u,v)&\alpha_{12}(u,v)\\
\alpha_{21}(u,v)&\alpha_{22}(u,v)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}x_{0}\\
x_{1}\end{bmatrix},\left[\dfrac{1}{v}:\dfrac{u}{v}:1\right]\right),\end{array}

where
\alpha_{ij}(u,v)\in\mathrm{k}[u,v]
are the polynomials satisfying

\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{11}(s+t,st)&\alpha_{12}(s+t,st)\\
\alpha_{21}(s+t,st)&\alpha_{22}(s+t,st)\end{bmatrix}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&-st\end{bmatrix}\text{ if }b=-1,\\
\dfrac{1}{s-t}\begin{bmatrix}s^{b}-t^{b}&st(s^{b-1}-t^{b-1})\\
s^{b+1}-t^{b+1}&st(s^{b}-t^{b})\end{bmatrix}\text{ if }b\geq 0.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{S}_{b}=\mathbb{P}(\kappa_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-m,0))
, where
m=b+1
and
\kappa
is given by

\kappa:\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2},\quad\big([y_{0}:y_{1}],[z_{0}:z_{1}]\big)\mapsto[y_{0}z_{0}:y_{0}z_{1}+y_{1}z_{0}:y_{1}z_{1}]

(see Definition
1.2.7
).
The preimages of
U_{0},U_{1}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
by
\kappa
are then two open subsets
T_{0}=\kappa^{-1}(U_{0})
and
T_{1}=\kappa^{-1}(U_{1})
of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
isomorphic to
\mathbb{A}^{2}
using the standard coordinates
s_{0}=\frac{y_{1}}{y_{0}},t_{0}=\frac{z_{1}}{z_{0}},s_{1}=\frac{y_{0}}{y_{1}},t_{1}=\frac{z_{0}}{z_{1}}
in
\mathrm{k}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1})
:

\begin{array}[]{l}T_{0}=\kappa^{-1}(U_{0})=\left\{\big([y_{0}:y_{1}],[z_{0}:z_{1}]\big)\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\mid y_{0}z_{0}\neq 0\right\}=\mathrm{Spec}\big(\mathrm{k}[s_{0},t_{0}]\big),\\[4.30554pt]
T_{1}=\kappa^{-1}(U_{1})=\left\{\big([y_{0}:y_{1}],[z_{0}:z_{1}]\big)\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\mid y_{1}z_{1}\neq 0\right\}=\mathrm{Spec}\big(\mathrm{k}[s_{1},t_{1}]\big).\end{array}

The line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-m,0)
is trivial on
T_{0}
and
T_{1}
,
so
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-m,0)
is the gluing of two copies of
\mathbb{A}^{3}
, via the transition function

\begin{array}[]{cccccc}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})\times\mathbb{A}^{1}&\xrightarrow{\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\simeq$}\vskip-2.0pt}}&(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\\
(s_{0},t_{0},a_{0})&\mapsto&\big(\frac{1}{s_{0}},\frac{1}{t_{0}},a_{0}s_{0}^{m}\big),\end{array}

which corresponds to the identifications
a_{1}=a_{0}s_{0}^{m}
,
s_{1}=\frac{1}{s_{0}}
,
t_{1}=\frac{1}{t_{0}}
. This transition function implies that a section on
T_{0}\cap T_{1}
correspond on the first chart to an element
f(s_{0},t_{0})\in\mathrm{k}[{s_{0}}^{\pm 1},{t_{0}}^{\pm 1}]
and on the second chart to
s_{1}^{-m}f(\frac{1}{s_{1}},\frac{1}{t_{1}})\in\mathrm{k}[{s_{1}}^{\pm 1},{t_{1}}^{\pm 1}]
.

To compute the transition on
U_{0}
and
U_{1}
, we take standard coordinates
u_{0}=\frac{Y}{X}
,
v_{0}=\frac{Z}{X}
,
u_{1}=\frac{Y}{Z}
,
v_{1}=\frac{X}{Z}
on
U_{0}=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{k}[u_{0},v_{0}])
and
U_{1}=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{k}[u_{1},v_{1}])
. We then observe that for
i=1,2
, the morphisms
\kappa_{|_{T_{i}}}:T_{i}\to U_{i}
corresponds to the injective algebra morphism
\mathrm{k}[U_{i}]=\mathrm{k}[u_{i},v_{i}]\hookrightarrow\mathrm{k}[T_{i}]=\mathrm{k}[s_{i},t_{i}]
that sends
u_{i}
and
v_{i}
onto
s_{i}+t_{i}
and
s_{i}t_{i}
, respectively.

The space of sections
\kappa_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-m,0)(U_{i})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-m,0)(T_{i})\simeq\mathrm{k}[T_{i}]=\mathrm{k}[s_{i},t_{i}]
is a free
\mathrm{k}[U_{i}]
-module of rank
2
generated by
1
and
\xi_{i}=s_{i}-t_{i}
. This basis being chosen, it determines a transition function, which is of the form

\begin{array}[]{ccc}U_{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{2}&\dashrightarrow&U_{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{2}\\
\left(u_{0},v_{0},\begin{bmatrix}b_{0}\\
b_{1}\end{bmatrix}\right)&\mapsto&\left(\dfrac{u_{0}}{v_{0}},\dfrac{1}{v_{0}},\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{11}(u_{0},v_{0})&\alpha_{12}(u_{0},v_{0})\\
\alpha_{21}(u_{0},v_{0})&\alpha_{22}(u_{0},v_{0})\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}b_{0}\\
b_{1}\end{bmatrix}\right)\end{array}

for some
\alpha_{ij}\in\mathrm{k}[u_{0},{v_{0}}^{\pm 1}]
. We then take a section on
U_{0}\cap U_{1}
, which is given on the first chart by
f_{0}(u_{0},v_{0}),f_{1}(u_{0},v_{0})\in\mathrm{k}[u_{0},{v_{0}}^{\pm 1}]
and on the second chart by

\begin{array}[]{rcl}g_{0}(u_{1},v_{1})&=&\alpha_{11}\big(\frac{u_{1}}{v_{1}},\frac{1}{v_{1}}\big)f_{0}\big(\frac{u_{1}}{v_{1}},\frac{1}{v_{1}}\big)+\alpha_{12}\big(\frac{u_{1}}{v_{1}},\frac{1}{v_{1}}\big)f_{1}\big(\frac{u_{1}}{v_{1}},\frac{1}{v_{1}}\big)\in\mathrm{k}[u_{1},{v_{1}}^{\pm 1}],\\
g_{1}(u_{1},v_{1})&=&\alpha_{21}\big(\frac{u_{1}}{v_{1}},\frac{1}{v_{1}}\big)f_{0}\big(\frac{u_{1}}{v_{1}},\frac{1}{v_{1}}\big)+\alpha_{22}\big(\frac{u_{1}}{v_{1}},\frac{1}{v_{1}}\big)f_{1}\big(\frac{u_{1}}{v_{1}},\frac{1}{v_{1}}\big)\in\mathrm{k}[u_{1},{v_{1}}^{\pm 1}].\end{array}

The corresponding section on
T_{0}\cap T_{1}
is then given on the two charts by

\begin{array}[]{rcll}f(s_{0},t_{0})&=&f_{0}(s_{0}+t_{0},s_{0}t_{0})+(s_{0}-t_{0})f_{1}(s_{0}+t_{0},s_{0}t_{0})&\in\mathrm{k}[s_{0}^{\pm 1},t_{0}^{\pm 1}],\\
g(s_{1},t_{1})&=&s_{1}^{-m}f\big(\frac{1}{s_{1}},\frac{1}{t_{1}}\big)\\
&=&g_{0}(s_{1}+t_{1},s_{1}t_{1})+(s_{1}-t_{1})g_{1}(s_{1}+t_{1},s_{1}t_{1})&\in\mathrm{k}[s_{1}^{\pm 1},t_{1}^{\pm 1}].\end{array}

We then use the equalities

\begin{array}[]{rcl}2g_{0}(s_{1}+t_{1},s_{1}t_{1})&=&g(s_{1},t_{1})+g(t_{1},s_{1})\in\mathrm{k}\big[s_{1}^{\pm 1},t_{1}^{\pm 1}\big],\\[4.30554pt]
2g_{1}(s_{1}+t_{1},s_{1}t_{1})&=&\frac{g(s_{1},t_{1})-g(t_{1},s_{1})}{s_{1}-t_{1}}\in\mathrm{k}[s_{1}^{\pm 1},t_{1}^{\pm 1}],\\[4.30554pt]
g_{0}\big(\frac{u_{0}}{v_{1}},\frac{1}{v_{1}}\big)&=&\alpha_{11}(u_{0},v_{1})f_{0}(u_{0},v_{1})+\alpha_{12}(u_{0},v_{1})f_{1}(u_{0},v_{1})\in\mathrm{k}[u_{0},{v_{1}}^{\pm 1}],\\[4.30554pt]
g_{1}\big(\frac{u_{0}}{v_{1}},\frac{1}{v_{1}}\big)&=&\alpha_{21}(u_{0},v_{1})f_{0}(u_{0},v_{1})+\alpha_{22}(u_{0},v_{1})f_{1}(u_{0},v_{1})\in\mathrm{k}[u_{0},{v_{1}}^{\pm 1}]\end{array}

to compute the
\alpha_{ij}
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}2g_{0}\big(\frac{s_{0}+t_{0}}{s_{0}},\frac{1}{s_{0}t_{0}}\big)&=&g\big(\frac{1}{s_{0}},\frac{1}{t_{0}}\big)+g\big(\frac{1}{t_{0}},\frac{1}{s_{0}}\big)=s_{0}^{m}f(s_{0},t_{0})+t_{0}^{m}f(t_{0},s_{0}),\\[4.30554pt]
&=&(s_{0}^{m}+t_{0}^{m})f_{0}(s_{0}+t_{0},s_{0}t_{0})+(s_{0}^{m}-t_{0}^{m})(s_{0}-t_{0})f_{1}(s_{0}+t_{0},s_{0}t_{0}),\\[4.30554pt]
2g_{1}\big(\frac{s_{0}+t_{0}}{s_{0}t_{0}},\frac{1}{s_{0}t_{0}}\big)&=&-\big(g\big(\frac{1}{s_{0}},\frac{1}{t_{0}}\big)-g\big(\frac{1}{t_{0}},\frac{1}{s_{0}}\big)\big)\frac{s_{0}t_{0}}{s_{0}-t_{0}}=-\big(s_{0}^{m}f(s_{0},t_{0})-t_{0}^{m}f(t_{0},s_{0})\big)\frac{s_{0}t_{0}}{s_{0}-t_{0}},\\[4.30554pt]
&=&-s_{0}t_{0}\frac{s_{0}^{m}-t_{0}^{m}}{s_{0}-t_{0}}f_{0}(s_{0}+t_{0},s_{0}t_{0})-s_{0}t_{0}(s_{0}^{m}+t_{0}^{m})f_{1}(s_{0}+t_{0},s_{0}t_{0})\end{array}

and get

\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{11}(s_{0}+t_{0},s_{0}t_{0})&\alpha_{12}(s_{0}+t_{0},s_{0}t_{0})\\
\alpha_{21}(s_{0}+t_{0},s_{0}t_{0})&\alpha_{22}(s_{0}+t_{0},s_{0}t_{0})\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}s_{0}^{m}+t_{0}^{m}&(s_{0}^{m}-t_{0}^{m})(s_{0}-t_{0})\\
-s_{0}t_{0}\frac{s_{0}^{m}-t_{0}^{m}}{s_{0}-t_{0}}&-s_{0}t_{0}(s_{0}^{m}+t_{0}^{m})\end{bmatrix}.

If
m=0
, we get simply the matrix defined above. If
m\geq 1
, we change the transition function, by observing that

\begin{array}[]{ll}\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{s+t}{st}\\
0&2\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}s^{m}+t^{m}&(s^{m}-t^{m})(s-t)\\
-st\frac{s^{m}-t^{m}}{s-t}&-st(s^{m}+t^{m})\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}2&s+t\\
0&-1\end{bmatrix}\\
=\dfrac{4st}{t-s}\begin{bmatrix}s^{m-1}-t^{m-1}&st(s^{m-2}-t^{m-2})\\
s^{m}-t^{m}&st(s^{m-1}-t^{m-1})\end{bmatrix}.\end{array}

∎

We then recover the following result already observed by Schwarzenberger
[
Sch61
, Proposition 7]
.

Corollary 4.2.2
.

We have the following isomorphisms of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundles:
\mathcal{S}_{-1}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}})
,
\mathcal{S}_{0}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{0}=\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{1}\simeq\mathbb{P}(T_{\mathbb{P}^{2}})
.

Proof.

Let us note that to obtain the transition function of the tangent bundle
T_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}
, it suffices to differentiate the map corresponding to the change of coordinates between two affine charts of
\mathbb{P}^{2}
. It follows that the transition function of the projectivised tangent bundle over
\mathbb{P}^{2}\setminus[0:1:0]
is

\begin{array}[]{rcccccc}&\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{0}\cap U_{1}&\xrightarrow{\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\simeq$}\vskip-2.0pt}}&\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{0}\cap U_{1}\\
&\big([x_{0}:x_{1}],[1:u:v]\big)&\mapsto&\big([x_{0}:x_{0}u+x_{1}v],\big[\frac{1}{v}:\frac{u}{v}:1\big]\big).\end{array}

Applying Proposition
4.2.1
, the transition functions for
b=-1,0,1
correspond, respectively, to the matrices

\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&-v\end{bmatrix}
,
\begin{bmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}
, and
\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
u&v\end{bmatrix}
, which gives the result.
∎

Remark 4.2.3
.

One can also see
\mathcal{S}_{1}\simeq\mathbb{P}(T_{\mathbb{P}^{2}})
as

Z=\left.\left\{\left([x_{0}:x_{1}:x_{2}],[y_{0}:y_{1}:y_{2}]\right)\in\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}\right|\sumop\displaylimits x_{i}y_{i}=0\right\},

with
\pi\colon\mathcal{S}_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
the projection onto the first factor. We again find the same transition function, by trivialising the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle over
U_{0}
and
U_{1}
via

\begin{array}[]{ccc}U_{0}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}&\rightarrow&Z\\
\big([1:u:v],[x_{0}:x_{1}]\big)&\mapsto&\big([1:u:v],[-x_{0}u-x_{1}v:x_{0}:x_{1}]\big),\\[4.30554pt]
U_{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}&\rightarrow&Z\\
\big([v:u:1],[x_{0}:x_{1}]\big)&\mapsto&\big([v:u:1],[-x_{1}:x_{0}:-x_{0}u+x_{1}v]\big).\end{array}

Seeing the equation with vectors, as
\vphantom{x}{}^{t}\!x\cdot y=0
, the group
\PGL_{3}
then acts via
(x,y)\mapsto(Ax,\vphantom{A}^{t}\!A^{-1}y)
.

We will see in Lemma
4.2.5
(2)
that
\Autz(\mathcal{S}_{1})\simeq\PGL_{3}
, so the above action yields all elements of
\Autz(\mathcal{S}_{1})
(but not of
\Aut(\mathcal{S}_{1})
, as we also have
(x,y)\mapsto(y,x)
).

Lemma 4.2.4
.

For each
b\geq 1
, the following hold:

(1)

The
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is isomorphic to

\mathcal{S}_{b}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}\big(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\big)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1},

obtained by pulling back the Schwarzenberger bundle
\mathcal{S}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
via the double cover
\kappa\colon\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
. In particular,
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
is isomorphic to

\mathbb{P}\big(\kappa^{*}\big(\kappa_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-b-1,0)\big)\big)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}.

(2)

The only curve invariant by
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})
is the curve
C\subset\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
, being the preimage of the curve
D\subset\mathcal{S}_{b}
given on the two charts of Lemma
4.2.1
by

\begin{array}[]{l}\left\{\left([x_{0}:x_{1}],([1:2t:t^{2}])\right)\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{0}\mid x_{0}+tx_{1}=0\right\},\\[4.30554pt]
\left\{\left([x_{0}:x_{1}],([t^{2}:2t:1])\right)\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{1}\mid x_{0}-tx_{1}=0\right\}.\end{array}

Proof.

(1)

We take as usual coordinates
([y_{0}:y_{1}],[z_{0}:z_{1}])
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
and denote by
T_{0},T_{1}\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
the open subsets given by, respectively,
y_{0}z_{0}\neq 0
and
y_{1}z_{1}\neq 0
.

The restriction of
\pi\colon\mathcal{S}_{b}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1})\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
to
T_{0}\cup T_{1}=\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\{([0:1],[1:0]),([1:0],[0:1])\}
is given by gluing
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{0}
and
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{1}
along
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{0}\cap T_{1}
by the isomorphism
\theta\in\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{0}\cap T_{1})
induced by

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{0}&\dashrightarrow&\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{1}\\[4.30554pt]
\left(\begin{bmatrix}x_{0}\\
x_{1}\end{bmatrix},\big([1:s],[1:t]\big)\right)&\mapsto&\left(\dfrac{1}{s-t}\begin{bmatrix}s^{b}-t^{b}&st(s^{b-1}-t^{b-1})\\
s^{b+1}-t^{b+1}&st(s^{b}-t^{b})\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}x_{0}\\
x_{1}\end{bmatrix},\left(\left[\dfrac{1}{s}:1\right],\left[\dfrac{1}{t}:1\right]\right)\right)\end{array}

(see Lemma
4.2.1
). We then define two open embeddings
\iota_{i}\colon\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
,
i=0,1
:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{0}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{0}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}&\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\\
\big([x_{0}:x_{1}],([1:s],[1:t])\big)&\mapsto&\big([-x_{0}-tx_{1}:x_{1};1:s],t\big),\\[4.30554pt]
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{1}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{1}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}&\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\\
\big([x_{0}:x_{1}],([s:1],[t:1])\big)&\mapsto&\big([-x_{0}+tx_{1}:x_{1};s:1],t\big)\end{array}

and compute
\iota_{1}\theta(\iota_{0})^{-1}\in\Bir(\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}&\iota_{1}\theta(\iota_{0})^{-1}&\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\\[4.30554pt]
\big([x_{0}:x_{1};1:y],z\big)&\mapsto&\left(\big[\frac{y^{b}}{z}x_{0}:\frac{y^{b+1}-z^{b+1}}{y-z}x_{0}+z^{b+1}x_{1};\frac{1}{y}:1\big],\frac{1}{z}\right)\\
&=&\left([x_{0}:x_{1}z^{b+2}+x_{0}z\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y^{i}z^{b-i};1:y],\frac{1}{z}\right)\end{array}

This then yields
\nu_{b,P}\iota_{0}=\iota_{1}\theta
, where
\nu_{b,P}\in\Aut(\mathbb{F}_{b}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
is given by

\nu_{b,P}\colon\big([x_{0}:x_{1};y_{0}:y_{1}],z\big)\mapsto\left([x_{0}:x_{1}z^{b+2}+x_{0}P(y_{0},y_{1},z);y_{0}:y_{1}],\frac{1}{z}\right),

with
P(y_{0},y_{1},z)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{b}y_{0}^{i}y_{1}^{b-i}P_{i}(z)z
and
P_{i}(z)=z^{i}\in\mathrm{k}[z]_{\leq b}
for
i=0,\ldots,b
. This shows that the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle is isomorphic to
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
over
T_{0}\cup T_{1}
(see Definition
3.5.1
) and thus over the whole
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
by Lemma
2.3.1
. This achieves the proof of
(1)
.

(2)
By Lemma
3.5.5
(3)
, there is a unique curve
C\subset\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
invariant by
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})
which corresponds to the intersection of
\pi^{-1}(\Delta)
with the surface
x_{0}=0
in both charts
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
. It then corresponds to the curve given in
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{0}
and
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{1}
by

\begin{array}[]{l}\big\{\big([x_{0}:x_{1}],([1:t],[1:t])\big)\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{0}\mid x_{0}+tx_{1}=0\big\},\\[4.30554pt]
\big\{\big([x_{0}:x_{1}],([t:1],[t:1])\big)\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\times T_{1}\mid x_{0}-tx_{1}=0\big\}.\end{array}

Sending this curve into
\mathcal{S}_{b}
yields a bijection
C\to D
, where
D\subset\mathcal{S}_{b}
is given locally as above.
∎

Lemma 4.2.5
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{S}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the
b
-th Schwarzenberger
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle, with
b\geq 1
, and let
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the conic which is the ramification locus of
\kappa\colon\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}

(
see Definition
\ref{def:Schwarz})
.

(1)

If
L\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a line, the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\pi^{-1}(L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}L\simeq\mathbb{P}^{1}
is isomorphic to

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathbb{F}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}&\text{ if $L$ is a tangent line to $\Gamma$;}\\
\mathbb{F}_{0}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}&\text{ if $L$ is a not a tangent line to $\Gamma$ and $b$ is even;\; or}\\
\mathbb{F}_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}&\text{ if $L$ is a not a tangent line to $\Gamma$ and $b$ is odd.}\end{array}

(2)

The action of
\Autz(\mathcal{S}_{b})
on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
yields a group isomorphism

\Autz(\mathcal{S}_{b})\xrightarrow{\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\simeq$}\vskip-2.0pt}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2},\Gamma)&=&\{g\in\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2})\mid g(\Gamma)=\Gamma\}\simeq\PGL_{2}&\text{ if }b\geq 2;\;or\\
\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2})&\simeq&\PGL_{3}&\text{ if }b=1.\end{array}\right.

Moreover, every automorphism of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\mathcal{S}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is trivial.

Proof.

Lemma
4.2.4
implies that
\hat{\pi}\colon\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{S}_{b}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1})\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}.
Lemma
3.5.5
(2)
then yields
\Autz(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})\simeq\PGL_{2}
, with an action on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
which is the diagonal one, and thus corresponds, via
\kappa\colon\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
, to
\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2},\Gamma)\simeq\PGL_{2}
. This implies, in particular, that the image of the group homomorphism
\Autz(\mathcal{S}_{b})\to\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2})
(given by Lemma
2.1.1
) contains
\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2},\Gamma)\simeq\PGL_{2}
.

Taking a line
L\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
tangent to
\Gamma
, the preimage
\kappa^{-1}(L)\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
consists of two fibres
f_{1},f_{2}
of the two projections, exchanged by the involution associated to the double cover
\kappa
. In particular,
\pi^{-1}(L)
is isomorphic to
\hat{\pi}^{-1}(f_{1})\simeq\hat{\pi}^{-1}(f_{2})\simeq\mathbb{F}_{b}
since
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b}
has numerical invariants
(0,b,b+2)
.

To finish the proof of
(1)
, we take a line
L\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
not tangent to
\Gamma
and show that
\pi^{-1}(L)
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{F}_{0}
or
\mathbb{F}_{1}
, depending on whether
b
is even or odd. Using the action of
\Autz(\mathcal{S}_{b})
, we can choose the line of equation
Y=0
. Using the notation of Proposition
4.2.1
, the restriction of
\mathcal{S}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
on
\mathbb{P}^{2}\setminus\{[0:1:0]\}
is given by gluing
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{0}
and
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{1}
with

\begin{array}[]{rcccccc}\theta\colon&\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{0}&\dashrightarrow&\mathbb{P}^{1}\times U_{1}\\
&\left(\begin{bmatrix}x_{0}\\
x_{1}\end{bmatrix},[1:u:v]\right)&\mapsto&\left(\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{11}(u,v)&\alpha_{12}(u,v)\\
\alpha_{21}(u,v)&\alpha_{22}(u,v)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}x_{0}\\
x_{1}\end{bmatrix},\big[\frac{1}{v}:\frac{u}{v}:1\big]\right),\end{array}

where
a_{ij}(u,v)\in\mathrm{k}[u,v]
are the polynomials satisfying

\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{11}(s+t,st)&\alpha_{12}(s+t,st)\\
\alpha_{21}(s+t,st)&\alpha_{22}(s+t,st)\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{s-t}\begin{bmatrix}s^{b}-t^{b}&st(s^{b-1}-t^{b-1})\\
s^{b+1}-t^{b+1}&st(s^{b}-t^{b})\end{bmatrix}.

We then observe that
s+t
divides
\frac{s^{n}-t^{n}}{s-t}
if
n
is even (by replacing
t
with
-s
), and that
\frac{s^{n}-t^{n}}{s-t}\equiv s^{n-1}\equiv\pm(st)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\pmod{s+t}
if
n
is odd. This yields

\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{11}(0,v)&\alpha_{12}(0,v)\\
\alpha_{21}(0,v)&\alpha_{22}(0,v)\end{bmatrix}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\begin{bmatrix}0&\pm v^{\frac{b}{2}}\\
\pm v^{\frac{b}{2}}&0\end{bmatrix}&\text{ if $b$ is even,}\\
\begin{bmatrix}\pm v^{\frac{b-1}{2}}&0\\
0&\pm v^{\frac{b+1}{2}}\end{bmatrix}&\text{ if $b$ is odd}\end{array}\right.

and achieves the proof of
(1)
.

To prove
(2)
, we study the group homomorphism
\rho\colon\Aut(\mathcal{S}_{b})\to\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2})
. As we observed, the image contains
\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2},\Gamma)\simeq\PGL_{2}
. It is then equal to
\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2},\Gamma)
if
b\geq 2
(this follows from
(1)
). If
b=1
, then
\rho
is surjective since
\mathcal{S}_{1}\simeq\mathbb{P}(T_{\mathbb{P}^{2}})
(see Corollary
4.2.2
and Remark
4.2.3
). To finish the proof of
(2)
, it remains to show that every automorphism
\alpha
of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!
-bundle
\mathcal{S}_{b}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is trivial. The lift of
\alpha
yields an element
\hat{\alpha}\in\Aut(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{b})
which acts trivially on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
and thus is trivial by Lemma
3.5.5
(2)
.
∎

Remark 4.2.6
.

Let
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the smooth conic of Definition
1.2.7
. Then the natural embedding of
\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2},\Gamma)\simeq\PGL_{2}
in
\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{2})=\PGL_{3}
is the one induced from the injective group homomorphism

(4.1)

\GL_{2}(k)\to\GL_{3}(k),\quad\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\mapsto\frac{1}{ad-bc}\begin{bmatrix}a^{2}&ab&b^{2}\\
2ac&ad+bc&2bd\\
c^{2}&cd&d^{2}\end{bmatrix}.

Also,
\PGL_{2}
acts on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
with two orbits, which are
\Gamma
and its complement. Indeed, the morphism
\kappa
of Definition
1.2.7
is
\PGL_{2}
-equivariant, where
\PGL_{2}
acts diagonally on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and as
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is the union of two orbits (namely, the diagonal and its complement), the result follows.

Remark 4.2.7
.

Let
m=b+1\geq 2
. By Lemma
4.2.5
, the group
\PGL_{2}
acts on
\mathcal{S}_{b}
, and the
